- 2026-07-05: Ingested [The Reasoning Trap: How Enhancing LLM Reasoning Amplifies Tool Hallucination](https://arxiv.org/html/2510.22977v2) (Chenlong Yin, Zeyang Sha, Shiwen Cui, Changhua Meng, Zechao Li, arXiv, 2026-04-17) — Yin et al. demonstrate a paradox: every technique that enhances LLM reasoning (RL, distillation, thinking toggles) also amplifies tool hallucination, causally tied to the reasoning step itself. The mechanistic analysis shows reasoning RL induces representational collapse on tool-related inputs, with late-layer residual streams as the locus where hallucinated responses become linearly separable. Mitigation via DPO cuts hallucination but degrades tool-use utility, revealing a fundamental reliability–capability trade-off. → raw/the-reasoning-trap.md, [[The Reasoning Trap]]. The accuracy/speed empirics are paywalled, but the conceptual framework is the clearest available. → raw/choosing-gguf-model-k-quants-i.md, [[Choosing a GGUF Model]]. Mohan, Don Haderle, Bruce Lindsay, Hamid Pirahesh, Peter Schwarz, ACM Transactions on Database Systems, 1992-03) — The paper that defined how every major database handles crash recovery: repeating-history paradigm, per-page LSNs, compensation log records with UndoNxtLSN chaining, and a three-pass restart (analysis→redo→undo). The architecture still runs inside PostgreSQL, SQL Server, DB2, and InnoDB three decades later. → raw/aries.md, [[ARIES — Write-Ahead Logging Recovery]]. - 2026-07-03: Ingested [Engineering for Bounded Cognition](https://shapeofthesystem.com/posts/2026/02/03/bounded-cognition) (Matt Williams, shapeofthesystem.com, 2026-02-03) — Working memory is ~4 chunks, attention is a torch beam, and unrehearsed info decays in 20 seconds. The gap between cognitive limits and million-line systems is the foundational problem of software. Naming, boundaries, tests, and undo as prosthetic cognition. LLMs have the same problem (Lost in the Middle). OXO Good Grips principle: design for the most constrained user. → raw/bounded-cognition.md, [[Engineering for Bounded Cognition]]. - 2026-06-22: Ingested [StoryScope: Investigating idiosyncrasies in AI fiction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03136) (Jenna Russell, Rishanth Rajendhran, Chau Minh Pham, Mohit Iyyer, John Wieting, 2026-04-03) — arXiv paper extracted via pdftotext. Pipeline extracts 304 discourse-level narrative features across 10 NarraBench dimensions from 61,608 stories (10,272 prompts × human + 5 LLMs). Narrative features alone achieve 93.2% macro-F1 for human vs. AI detection, retaining 97% of performance with stylistic cues added. 30 core features capture the signal: AI over-explains themes, favors tidy plots, renders emotion through bodies; humans are temporally complex, morally ambiguous, reader-aware. Per-model fingerprints (Claude flat escalation, GPT gossip, Gemini bleakness). AI stories converge on shared narrative region; human stories rarer and more dispersed. Features survive stylistic editing (only 1.6pp F1 drop). → raw/storyscope.md, [[StoryScope]]. - 2026-06-21: Ingested [The Market for Doom: Why We Keep Predicting the End of Work](https://rogerpartridge.substack.com/p/the-market-for-doom-why-we-keep-predicting) (Roger Partridge, Substack, 2026-06-12) — Full article via WebFetch. Partridge traces a lineage of failed doomsayers (Rifkin, Ehrlich, Club of Rome) and argues the cognitive error is static-vs-dynamic reasoning: doom predictions project current conditions forward without accounting for new industries, occupations, and wants that emerge from displacement. Key concepts: the ATM counterexample, horses as the only species that couldn't retrain, Bastiat's seen-vs-unseen, institutional preconditions as the real variable. Closes by inverting "dismal science" — economists who take scarcity and adaptation seriously have been the optimists all along. → raw/the-market-for-doom.md, [[The Market for Doom]]. - 2026-06-15: Ingested [Chunker](https://github.com/sermakarevich/chunker) (sermakarevich, 2025) — Full repo analysis. Python CLI (~2,700 LOC) that builds navigable knowledge trees from documents via LLM-mediated semantic chunking and bottom-up hierarchical aggregation. Architecture: cursor-driven Phase 1 (expand window until LLM finds topic boundary, verbatim phrase validation with retry), bottom-up Phase 2 (contiguous grouping with hard/soft validation, recursive synthesis), priority-ordered context injection with all-or-nothing insertion, atomic checkpointing via mkstemp+rename, integrated PDF pipeline via PyMuPDF rendering + vision model rewrite. Key decisions: LLM boundary detection over fixed-size splitting, contiguous grouping over arbitrary clustering, hard min_group_size=2 to prevent degenerate single-child blocks, vision model only at rewrite (not at chunking) for token efficiency. → raw/sermakarevich-chunker.md, [[Chunker (Document Chunking Tool)]]. - 2026-06-21: Ingested [Inference cost at scale with napkin math](https://injuly.in/blog/napkin-inference-cost/index.html) (injuly.in, 2026-06-14) — Napkin math for LLM serving economics: memory bandwidth as the real bottleneck (B200 compute sits idle 98% of the time), KV-cache hit rate as margin, duty cycle as the 5x multiplier nobody measures. Starting from GPU specs and matrix multiplication cost, derives the dollar cost per user ($9.36/month at $4/hr rental, $133/user lifetime at $40K ownership). Covers the bandwidth-compute balancing equation (2B = 562 → B ≈ 331 theoretical users), VRAM as the real constraint (GQA KV-cache: ~26GB/user → ~6 concurrent at full context), and PagedAttention + idle time giving 300-800 practical users per B200. → raw/napkin-inference-cost.md, [[Inference Cost Napkin Math]]. Core insight: inventive problems and solutions follow repeatable patterns across domains. Tools: 40 inventive principles, contradiction matrix, Ideal Final Result, ARIZ algorithm, substance-field analysis. Documented ROI: Intel $212.5M (21 months), Samsung 50 patents/year. Modern variants: TOP-TRIZ, I-TRIZ, Liberating Structures' TRIZ. → raw/wikipedia-triz.md, [[TRIZ]]. - 2026-06-11: Ingested [TriadJS](https://github.com/justhamade/triadjs) (justhamade) — Full repo analysis. TypeScript API framework (21 packages, 4 examples, 83+ BDD scenarios, 1000+ tests) where specification, implementation, validation, and tests are the same source of truth. Core: immutable schema DSL (`SchemaNode<TOutput>`) with 14 primitive kinds, `t.model()`, and DDD composition (pick/omit/partial/extend/merge). Key innovations: scenario.auto() for schema-derived adversarial test generation, `Symbol.for()` brands for cross-module-graph identity (jiti-compatible), phantom state witness pattern for Channel type inference, kind-based structural discrimination over instanceof, dialect-neutral codegen IR for database schemas. Generates OpenAPI 3.1, AsyncAPI 3.0, Gherkin, typed frontend hooks (React/Solid/Vue/Svelte), WebSocket clients, Drizzle schemas, form validators, and breaking-change detection. Ships as a Claude Code plugin with 10 skills and 8 slash commands. → raw/triadjs.md, [[TriadJS]]. ## [2026-05-14] ingest | SAM Audio (Meta AI Research)

Source: https://ai.meta.com/research/samaudio/ — Meta's foundation model for prompted audio separation in the Segment Anything family. Text, visual, span, and multi-modal prompts isolate any sound from audio or audio-visual sources. Flow-matching Diffusion Transformer, open weights under SAM License, companion judge model correlated with human evaluation. Created `raw/sam-audio.md` and wiki page `SAM Audio.md`. Added to index under AI Research & Models. Cross-linked to [[Local and Open Source Inference]], [[2025 in LLMs]], [[A Non-Anthropomorphized View of LLMs]].

## [2026-05-14] ingest | Long Live Systems of Record (Clouded Judgement / Jamin Ball)

Source: https://cloudedjudgement.substack.com/p/clouded-judgement-121225-long-live — Jamin Ball refutes "systems of record are dead" takes, reframing SOR as "where does the truth live." Agents are inherently cross-system and action-oriented; they raise standards for SORs rather than replacing them. Databricks bull thesis, warehouse-as-truth-registry, AI wrappers as clean contracts. Created `raw/clouded-judgement-121225-long-live.md` and wiki page `Long Live Systems of Record.md`. Added to index under Databases & Data (after Materialized Views Are Obviously Useful). Cross-linked to [[AI Killing B2B SaaS]] (complementary SOR survival thesis), [[Smart Models Dumb Pipes]] (agents as judgment, infrastructure as execution), [[Materialized Views Are Obviously Useful]] (database as authoritative derived-data layer), [[Databases and Data]] (synthesis), [[Computer Use is 45x More Expensive Than Structured APIs]] (API-first access economics), [[DAB]] (auto-generated APIs over databases), [[Two Kinds of User Are Emerging]] (legacy SaaS as bottleneck), [[The Future of Software Engineering is SRE]] (infrastructure as differentiator), [[Specifications as the Product]] (durable artifacts over disposable execution), [[Building Agents for Production Systems with MCP]] (agent-to-system connectivity).

## [2026-05-14] ingest | Coding Agents & Complexity Budgets (Lee Robinson)

Source: https://leerob.com/agents -- Lee Robinson's field report on migrating cursor.com off a headless CMS back to code/Markdown using Cursor's coding agents: a weekend, $260 in tokens, 344 agent requests. The "complexity budget" thesis: anything between an agent and grep-able source code is a tax on productivity. Created `raw/coding-agents-and-complexity-budgets.md` and wiki page `Coding Agents and Complexity Budgets.md`. Added to index under Agentic Development (between Building low-level software and RepoMirror). Cross-linked to [[Simplicity in the Age of AI-Assisted]] (demolition over construction), [[Agent Coding Workflow]] (generation vs verification), [[Compound Engineering]] (screenshot comparison as automated verification), [[Cognitive Debt]] (removing Storybook on impulse), [[Radical Accountability]] (taste as differentiator), [[RepoMirror]] (parallel migration story), [[Code Field]] (resist over-specification), [[The Plan Is the Program]] (plan as atomic unit), [[Specifications as the Product]] (code is disposable), [[Guardrails and Feedback Loops]] (absent verification rigor), [[Talking to Transformers]] (complexity budget as attention budget parallel), [[The Mythical Agent-Month]] (agents generate new complexity), [[On a Year of Multi-Model Development]] (another field report), [[acceleration-flow]] (nerd-sniped by dopamine loop).

## [2026-05-14] synthesis | 11 synthesis pages created
Created 11 cross-cutting synthesis pages from 197 individual wiki pages. Each synthesis page opens with a thesis, maps the landscape of tools and techniques, identifies 3-5 key tensions, notes gaps, and cross-links heavily to individual pages and other synthesis pages.

Pages created: Agent Coding Workflow, Specifications as the Product, Guardrails and Feedback Loops, Agent Memory and Context, Agent Orchestration, Personal Agents, Security and Sandboxing, Local and Open Source Inference, Software Engineering Craft, Databases and Data, Distributed Systems.

Updated index.md with a new Synthesis section at the top listing all 11 pages with one-line descriptions. Read all 100+ individual pages before writing to ensure synthesis was informed by actual content, not just index summaries.

## [2026-05-14] ingest | json-render (Vercel Labs)

Source: https://json-render.dev/ — Vercel Labs' open-source generative UI framework. LLMs output JSON constrained to a Zod-defined component catalog; components render progressively as the stream arrives. 41 built-in components, React and React Native, code export to standalone apps. 15k stars. Created `raw/json-render.md` and wiki page `json-render.md`. Added to index under Developer Tools (next to smui). Cross-linked to [[Spec-First Development at Benchling]] (define-once schema pattern), [[vibes-cli]] (opposing philosophy for AI-generated UI), [[Guardrails and Feedback Loops]] (structured output as guardrail), [[smui]] (shadcn/ui component theming), [[Pretext]] (JS/TS UI tooling).

Key findings across the synthesis: (1) The planner/worker/judge pattern emerges independently across Cursor, Hightouch, and Maestro -- convergence on a correct solution. (2) Linters beat prompts as the universal guardrail insight. (3) Context management, not model ability, is the real engineering bottleneck. (4) Agent orchestration is reinventing distributed systems primitives that BEAM/OTP solved in 1986. (5) The wiki is thinnest on distributed systems, C#/.NET, and failure case studies.

## [2026-05-14] ingest | TextForge Case Study (Aaron Stannard)

Source: https://aaronstannard.com/software-2.0-case-study-textforge/ — Stannard walks through building TextForge (AI email automation, passed Google CASA2 audit) with Claude Code writing nearly all the code. Six-layer discipline: planning mode first (400-line CLAUDE.md before any code), reference architectures (DrawTogether.NET as structural template), agent skills (incremental codification), fine-grained PRDs (token permissions with human-only approval scopes), evolving verification pipeline (Roslyn analyzers to snapshot tests via Verify library), and periodic refactoring to vertical slices. Created `raw/software-2.0-case-study-textforge.md` and wiki page `TextForge Case Study.md`. Added to index under Agentic Development (before Claude Code on the Go). Cross-linked to [[Compound Engineering]] (six layers as compound loop), [[Specifications as the Product]] (CLAUDE.md and PRDs as durable artifacts), [[Ralph]] (autonomous loop for next phase), [[Feedback Loop is All You Need]] (verification pipeline as core discipline), [[Harness Engineering]] (pipeline evolution maps to Böckeler's framework), [[dotnet Slopwatch]] (.NET anti-cheat complement), [[Slowing the Fuck Down]] (planning mode as deliberate friction), [[Radical Accountability]] (taste is what's left), [[Guardrails and Feedback Loops]] (verification philosophy), [[Agent Coding Workflow]] (maturity spectrum placement), [[MinMax Skills]] (skill library pattern), [[How Boris Uses Claude Code]] (creator vs. practitioner), [[Pre-Commit Lint Checks]] (linting infrastructure evolved past).

## [2026-05-14] ingest | Writing a Good CLAUDE.md (Kyle / HumanLayer)

Source: https://www.humanlayer.dev/blog/writing-a-good-claude-md — HumanLayer's guide to CLAUDE.md authorship. Core argument: CLAUDE.md is the highest-leverage file in the agent harness, so keep it short (<300 lines), universally applicable, hand-crafted (never /init), and free of style rules that belong in linters. ## [2026-05-15] ingest | agentsh (agentsh.org + GitHub README)

Source: https://www.agentsh.org/docs/ + https://github.com/canyonroad/agentsh — Eran Sandler's execution-layer security gateway for AI agents. Sits at the syscall boundary (FUSE + eBPF + seccomp on Linux) enforcing allow/deny/approve/redirect policies on every file op, network connection, process spawn, and signal. The distinguishing feature is `redirect` as a first-class policy primitive: instead of denying and triggering agent retry loops, transparently swap commands, file paths, DNS responses, and TCP connections. Includes embedded LLM proxy with DLP, MCP-native security controls (tool whitelisting, version pinning, cross-server exfiltration blocking), profile-then-lock policy generation, and session reports. macOS is second-class (ESF doesn't support transparent redirect). Created `raw/agentsh.md` and wiki page `agentsh.md`. Added to index under Security & Sandboxing (after VTcode). Updated [[Security and Sandboxing]] synthesis page: added execution-layer enforcement section, updated "What's Missing" sections on network firewalls and MCP-native security with agentsh references. Source: https://github.com/hyhmrright/brooks-lint — Full repo clone and deep analysis. AI code review plugin (v1.3.0, MIT) that diagnoses code against 12 decay risks synthesized from 12 classic software engineering books (Brooks, McConnell, Fowler, Martin, Hunt & Thomas, Evans, Ousterhout, Winters et al., Meszaros, Osherove, Feathers, Google Testing). Pure prompt-engineering architecture: no parsing, no AST, no static analysis — the product is markdown files concatenated into LLM system prompts. Six modes (PR review, architecture audit, tech debt, test quality, health dashboard, full sweep with auto-fix). Key innovations: Iron Law diagnosis chain, "What Not to Flag" false-positive guards per risk, eval-first development with 47 scenarios, project-level YAML config, trend tracking. Created `raw/brooks-lint.md` and wiki page `brooks-lint.md`. Added to index under Quality & Guardrails.
